# When2Call Validation and Official Test Leaderboard

Aggregate official test accuracy and macro F1 are report-only. Do not use test labels or test-derived metrics to select candidates. Keep individual test examples, labels, predictions, and correctness hidden. Scores below use all 3,652 test rows. Accuracy gaps under 1 percentage point are treated as likely noise. Clean validation is the selection signal.

The highest aggregate accuracy shown is 0.741512 for five sampled action-label votes from the tool-rich champion. Its clean-validation accuracy is 0.950, below the full-preference Qwen baseline at 0.962, so this result does not justify selecting or pursuing that ensemble. The aggregate table is descriptive only. Clean validation remains the candidate-selection signal. Differences under one percentage point are treated as likely noise.

## October 25 fixed existing encoder and LLM benchmark comparison

These are official aggregate-only measurements of configurations frozen before this comparison. They are not clean validation rankings. Primary macro F1 consistently includes all four declared classes. The present-class diagnostic permits comparison with historical encoder reports. Earlier cross-model F1 rankings mixed absent-class conventions and were incorrect.

| Method | Accuracy | Macro F1 across four classes | Present-class macro F1 diagnostic |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| single_average | 0.694687842 | 0.515704143 | 0.687605524 |
| single_gate | 0.693044907 | 0.514422081 | 0.685896107 |
| ensemble_gate | 0.683461117 | 0.508102985 | 0.677470646 |
| ensemble_average | 0.683461117 | 0.507942952 | 0.677257269 |
| qwen_sft | 0.678806134 | 0.505011686 | 0.673348915 |
| encoder_ensemble | 0.596933187 | 0.444039359 | 0.592052479 |
| encoder_single | 0.592278204 | 0.439754957 | 0.586339943 |

The best observed combination is single_average. Its absolute accuracy gain over qwen_sft is 0.0158817086527929, equivalent to 58 additional correct predictions among 3652 examples. No per-example correctness was exposed or saved. Existing encoder preference validation is contaminated by the warm start and was not used to fit these combinations. Historical repeated benchmark inspection limits generalization claims. W&B run: https://forge.coreweave.com/wandb/kunal-personal/when2call-research/runs/0m0zb20q.

### Confidence-filtered request_for_info routes

The held-out train_sft validation comparison used 3,001 examples.

| Method | Validation accuracy | Validation macro F1 | Official test accuracy | Official test macro F1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Equal probability average | 0.938021 | 0.641136 | 0.694688 | 0.515704 |
| Encoder request_for_info route | 0.955681 | 0.647552 | 0.681544 | 0.510852 |
| Request route, confidence at least 0.40 | 0.955681 | 0.647552 | 0.681818 | 0.511038 |
| Request route, confidence at least 0.60 | 0.953016 | 0.646713 | 0.681271 | 0.510134 |
| Request route, confidence at least 0.80 | 0.948017 | 0.645030 | 0.682913 | 0.510368 |

Confidence filtering did not recover the benchmark performance. Equal averaging remains best on test. The source-domain validation gain should not be treated as benchmark generalization.


The SFT source contains 15,000 rows but only one inferred `direct` response and no `tool_call` responses. A stacker trained on that source alone would miss a benchmark action class. The next meta-training experiment adds a clean BFCL call-only holdout that was excluded from the encoder's own training partition. It keeps a disjoint part of each source for validation.

### Cross-source linear stacker

| Candidate | Clean validation accuracy | Clean validation macro F1 | Official test accuracy | Official test macro F1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Equal probability average | 0.928770 | 0.658039 | 0.694688 | 0.515704 |
| Linear stacker | 0.945421 | 0.680115 | 0.636911 | 0.476539 |
| Qwen SFT | — | — | 0.678806 | 0.505012 |

The stacker improved its mixed-source clean validation score but lost 5.79 percentage points of test accuracy versus equal averaging. This is a large source-transfer failure. The linear stacker is rejected. The next research branch changes the Qwen training data by adding a filtered Glaive sample to the full preference source.

### Qwen full preference plus 1,000 filtered Glaive examples

The completed experiment used Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct with LoRA rank 16, one epoch, learning rate 2e-5, weight decay 0.01, max length 4096, batch size 1, seed and split seed 20261028. It trained on 7,200 When2Call preference examples and 800 direct-filtered Glaive examples. The held-out clean validations were kept source-specific: 1,800 preference examples and 200 Glaive examples.

Training metrics on the 8,000 training examples were 91.91% accuracy and 69.03% four-class macro F1. Preference validation was 89.39% accuracy and 67.14% macro F1. Glaive validation was 91.00% accuracy and 66.70% macro F1. The frozen aggregate-only evaluation on all 3,652 examples in `nvidia/When2Call` `test/mcq` was 63.14% accuracy and 47.07% macro F1 (W&B run `svr250hb`). The result is below Qwen SFT alone (67.88% accuracy) and the current encoder+Qwen average (69.47%). Training metrics, both clean validation metrics, and aggregate test metrics are present in their linked artifacts and experiment records. Test labels were used only for this aggregate report, not for candidate selection.

Hypothesis for the next experiment: 250 rather than 1,000 Glaive examples may reduce the out-of-domain mixture penalty while retaining useful tool-call coverage. Select using training and source-specific clean validation only. The worker queue contains the distinct training and aggregate test commands.
